का . पा . 3186. - - प्रायकर अधिनियम , 1961 ( 1961 का 43 ) की धारा 120 की उपधारा ( 1 ) 
एवं ( 2 ) में दिए गए अधिकार , अधिसूचना संख्या 1965 फाइल सं . 279/ 93 - प्राई . टी . जे . ( भाग - II ) दिनांक 1- 7- 04 

और एस मो . सं . 504 दिनांक , 5- 7- 94 के अनुसार के . प्र . क , बोई, नई दिल्ली , द्वारा दिए गए अधिकार एवं मझे 
मोरे गाए अन्य अधिकारों एवं . इम मंदर्भ में पूर्व अधिसूचनाओं का अधिक्रमण एवं प्रांशिक संशोधन करते हुए , 

से अधिक्रमण के पहले किए गए मामलों या किए जाने वाले मामलों को छोड़कर, मैं , मुख्य प्रायकर आयुक्त- II , 
कलकत्ता , एतवद्वारा यह आदेश देता है कि संलग्न अनुसूची के कालम 2 " में विनिर्दिष्ट म क्षेत्र के ग्रायकर 
प्रायक्त ( अ ) ऐसे व्यक्तियों के संबंध में अपने कृत्य का पालन करेंगे जिनके प्रायकर अथवा धनकर या दानकर 
या अतिकर या ब्याजकर या व्ययकर या मंपदा शुल्क का निर्धारण कालम 3 में विनिर्दिष्ट श्रायकर प्राधि 
काग्यिोनिर्धारण अधिकारियों के द्वारा प्रायकर अधिनियम 1961 की धारा 246 की उपधारा ( 2 ) के खंड 
( ए ) में ( एच ) तक , धनकर अधिनियम , 1957 ( 1957 का 27 ) की धारा 23 की उपधारा ( 1ए ) के खण्ड 
( ए) में (ई ) तक , दानकर अधिनियम , 1958 ( 1958 का 18 ) की धारा 22 ( 1ए ) के खण्ड ( ए ) से ( ई ) 
तक , ( कम्पनी लाभ ) प्रतिकर अधिनियम 1984 ( 1984 का 7 ) की धारा 11 की उपधारा ( 1 ) व्याजकर अधि 
नियम 1974 ( 1974 का 45 ) की धारा 15 की उपधारा ( 1 ) और ब्याजकर अधिनियम , 1987 ( 1987 का 
२८ ) की धारा 22 वी उपधारा ( 1 ) और मम्पदा शल्क अधिनियम 1953 की धारा 62 में उल्लिखित किन्हीं

दूसरे प्रभार में स्थानान्तरित हो गए हों , इम अधिसूचना के जारी होने के त 
के समीप उन प्रायकर वार्ड सकिल विशेष रेंज अथवा उनके अंश में हुई निर्धारण में उदभत अपील लंबित हों 
तो इस अधिसूचना के लागू होने की तिथि में उन विशेष वार्ड/ मकिल विशेष रेंज अथवा उनके अंश से स्थानान्त 
रित किए गए मामलों का निष्पावन उन प्रायकर आयुक्त ( अ ) के द्वारा किए जायेंगे , जिनके अधीन उक्त वार्ड

MINISTRY OF FINANCE 
OFFICE OF THE CHIEF COMMISSIONER OF INCOME- TAX II 

NOTIFICATION NO . 1/97- 98 : 

Calcutta, the 19th September, 1997 
S .O . 3186 . - In exorcise of the powers conferred by the sub -section (1) & (2 )of section 120 of the Income Tax 
Act,1961(43 of 1961 ) and the powers conferred on me by the Central Board of Direct Taxes, New Delhi, vide Noti 
ficationNo. 9565 F .No. 279 /93 -ITJ( Pt,II)dated 5 -7 - 1994 and S. O . No.504 dated 5 -7- 1994 and all other powers enabl . 
ing me in this behalf and in partial modification and in supersession of all carlier Notifications made in this behalf , 
except in respect of thing done or omitted to be done before such supersession ,1, the Chief Commissioner of Incomo 
tax II, Calcutte, hereby direct that the Commissioners of Income Tax (Appeals )of this reason specified in column 2 
of the schedule attached hereto , shall perform their functions in respect of such persons assessod to Income Tax 
or Wealth Tax or Gift Tax or Sur-tax or Interest Tax or Expenditure Tax or Estate Duty by the Income Tax Autho 
rities Assessing Officers specified in column 3 thereof as are aggrieved by any orders mentioned in clauses (a ) to 
(h ) of sub - section (2) of section 246 of the Income Tax Act, 1961, clauses (a ) to (e) of sub -section (1A ) of section 
23 of the Wealth -tax Act, 1957 (27 of 1957), clauses (a ) to (e ) of sub -section (1A ) of section 22 of the Gift -tax , Act , 
1958 (18 of 1958 ), sub - section (1) of section 11 of the Companies (Profit) Sur- tax Act, 1984 (7 of 1984 ), sub -section 
(1 ) of section 15 of the Interest-tax Act, 1974 (45 of 1974 ), sub -section (1) of section 22 of the Expenditure-tax Act, 
1987 (35 of 1987 ) and section 62 of the Estate Duty Act, 1953. 

2 . Where an Income-tax Circle , Ward of D .C . Range or Special Rango or part thereof stands transferred by 
this Notification from one charge to another, appoals arising out of the assossments made in that Income-tax Ward 
Circle/ Special Range or part thereof and pending, immediately before the date from which this Notification takes 
effect, before the Commissioner of Income-tax (Appeals ) from whose charge that particular Income-tax Ward / 
Circle /Special Range or part thereof is transferred , shall from the date from which this Notification takes effoct, 
be transferred to and dealt with by the Commissioners of Income-tax (Appeals ) to whom the said Ward / Circle/ 
Spocial Range or part thereof is transferred . 
3. This Notification takes offect from 22 - 9- 1997.

का० प्रा० 3192 . -- केन्द्रीय मरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि इससे उपाबद्ध अनुसूची में उल्लिम्बित भूमि में कोयला 
अभिप्राप्त किए जाने की संभावना है , 


अतः, अब , केन्द्रीय सरकार कोयला धारक क्षेत्र ( अर्जन और विकास ) अधिनियम , 1957 ( 1957 का 20 ) (जिसे इसमें 
इसके पश्चात् उक्त अधिनियम कहा गया है ) की धारा 4 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रवन शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, उस क्षेत्र 
में कोयले का पूर्वेक्षण करने के अपने आशय की सूचना देती है , 


इस अधिसूचना के अंतर्गत आने वाले रेखांक सं० ईसीएल केबीए/ एलए केओपी/ 1/ 97, तारीख 23 जून , 1997 का निरीक्षण 

न कार्यालय सेंक्टोरिया डाकघर-दिमेरगढ़ - 713333, जिला , वर्दबान ( पश्चिम बंगाल ) के कार्यालय 
में या कलक्टर , जिला बांकुरा ( पश्चिम बंगाल ) के कार्यालय में या कोयला नियंत्रक , 1, काउंसिल हाउस स्ट्रीट , कलकता 700001 
के कार्यालय में किया जा सकता है । 


इस अधिसूचना के अंतर्गत आने वाली भूमि में , हितबद्ध सभी व्यक्ति उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 13 की उपधाग ( 7 ) में 
निर्दिष्ट सभी नक्शों , पार्टी और अन्य दस्तावेजों को इस अधिसूचना के राजपत्र में प्रकाशन की तारीख में नब्बे दिन के भीतर भार 
साधक अधिकारी विभागाध्यक्ष/ ( भू - राजस्व ) ईस्टर्न कोलफील्डम लिमिटेड , म्म्यालय सैक्टोरिया , डाकघर-दिमेरगढ़ -713333 , जिला 
वर्दमान (पश्चिम बंगाल ) को भेजेंगे ।

क - ख : रेखा बिन्दु, "क " से प्रारंभ होती है और ग्राम/मौजा साहेबडांगा अधिकारिता सूची सं० 45 से होकर गुजरती है तथा 

बिन्दु “ ख ” बिन्दु पर मिलती है । 
रेखा जिन्दु “ ख ” से प्रारंभ होती है तथा ग्राम/ मौजा भाड़ा अधिकारिता सूची सं० 1 से होकर गुजरती है और बिन्दु 
" ग " पर मिलती है । 
रेखा बिन्दु “ग ” से प्रारंभ होती है और ग्राम / मौजा कालीदासपुर अधिकारिता सूची सं० 2 से होकर गुजरती है तथा 
बिन्दु “ घ ” पर मिलती है । 
रेखा बिन्दु “ब” से प्रारंभ होती है और ग्राम/ मौजा जुगीबाग अधिकारिता सूची सं० 15 से होकर गुजरती है तथा 
बिन्दु ङ पर मिलती है । 
रेग्ना विन्दु “कु ” से प्रारंभ होती है और ग्राम /मौजा अधिकारिता सूची सं० 14 से होकर गुजरती है तथा बिन्दु “च " 
पर मिलती है । 
रेखा बिन्दु "च " से प्रारंभ होती है और ग्राम / मौजा घुसरा अधिकारिता सूची सं० 13 से गुजरती है तथा बिन्दु “ छ " 

पर मिलती है । 
छ - ज : रेखा बिन्दु “ छ ” से प्रारंभ होती है और ग्राम/ मौजा जोरसा अधिकारिता सूची सं० 18 से होकर गुजरती है तथा 

ग्राम/ मौजा घुसरा अधिकारिता सूची सं० 13 में बिन्दु "ज " पर मिलती है । 
ज -- श : रेखा बिन्दु " म " से आरंभ होती है और ग्राम मौजा घुसरा अधिकारिता सूची सं० 13 से होकर गुजरती है तथा 

बिन्दु “ श " पर मिलती है । 
रेखा बिन्दु " म " से प्रारंभ होती है और ग्राम मौजा कसीराईतोरे अधिकारिता सूची सं० 12 से होकर गुजरती है 

तथा बिम्दु " म " पर मिलती है ।

रेखा ग्राम सामांगी में सड़क की बाहरी सीमा के साथ-साथ होकर जाती है और बिन्दु " श " पर मिलती है ।

फिर प्लाट सं० 140, 141 की बाहरी सीमा के साथ-साथ जाती है और बिन्दु “ ज " पर मिलती है । 
रेखा सामांगी और सेनोली ग्रामों की सम्मिलित ग्राम सीमा के साथ -साथ जाती है और बिन्दु "ट " पर मिलती है । 
रेखा साआंगो और करटो ग्रामों को सम्मिलित ग्राम सोना के साथ- साथ जाती है और प्रारंभिक बिन्दु "क " पर मिलती

मा०मा० 3198 . - ~- केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि इससे उपायद अनुसूची में उल्लिखित भूमि में कोयला 
अभिप्राप्त किए जाने की संभावना है , 

प्रतः, अन , केन्द्रीय सरकार कोयला धारक क्षेत्र ( अर्जन और विकास ) अधिनिमम 1957 ( 1957 का 20 ) (जिसे इसमें 
इसके पश्चात् उक्त अधिनियम कहा गया है ) धारा 4 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदन शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, उम क्षेत्र में 
कोयले का पूर्वेक्षण करने के अपने प्राशय की सूचना देती है , 

इस अधिसूचना के अंतर्गत पाने पाले रेखांक सं० ई सी एल/ पार जे०एम०एल० जी०एम /एल०ए० / 63 तारीख 20 जुलाई, 
1996 का निरीक्षण उपायुक्त जिला गोडा ( बिहार ) के कार्यालय में या कोयला नियंत्रक, 1 काउंसिल हाउस स्ट्रीट , कलकत्ता 
100001 के कार्यालय में या निदेशक तकनीकी ( पश्चिम प्रभाग ) ईस्टर्न कोलफील्डस लिमिटेड, मेक्टोरिया डाकघर-दिशेरगढ़ (जिला 
बर्दवान ) के कार्यालय में किया जा सकता है । 

इस अधिसूचमा के अंतर्गत आने वाली भूमि में , हितबद्ध सभी व्यक्ति उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 13 की उपधारा ( 7 ) में 
निविष्ट सभी नक्शों, चाटों और अन्य दस्तावेजों को इस अधिसूचना के राजपत्र में प्रकाशन की तारीख से नब्बे दिन के भीतर निदेशक 
तकनीक ( पश्चिम प्रभाग ) ईस्टर्न कोलफील्ड्स लिमिटेड, सेक्टोरिया , डाकघर-दिगेरगढ़ जिला बर्दवान ( पश्चिमी बंगाल ) को भेजेंगे ।

का . आ . 3205... - केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि लोकहित में ऐसा करना आवश्यक है कि पश्चिमी 
बंगाल राज्य के हल्दिया से बिहार राज्य के बरौनी तक पैट्रोलियम उत्पाद के परिवहन के लिए इंडियन प्रायल कारपोरेशन 
लिमिटेड द्वारा पाईपलाईन बिछाई जानी चाहिए 

और यह प्रतीत होता है कि उक्त पाईपलाईन बिछा ने के प्रयोजन के लिये इस अधिसूचना से उपाबद्ध अनुसूची में 
वणित भमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन करना आवश्यक है ; 

अतः, अब , केन्द्रीय सरकार , पैट्रोलियम और खनिज पाईप लाईन ( भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का प्रर्जन ) अधिनियम, 
1962( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की उपधारा ( 1 )द्वारा प्रदत शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए उनमें उपयोग के अधिकार 
का अर्जन करने के अपने आशय की घोषणा करती है । 

उक्त अनुसूची में वणित भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई व्यक्ति उस तारीख से जिसको राजपन्न में यथा प्रकाशित इस 
अधिसचना की प्रतियां साधारण जनता को उपलब्ध करा दी जाती है , इक्कीस दिन के भीतर भमि के नीचे पापिलान 
बिछाने के संबंध में या उनमें उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन करने संबंधी लिखित रूप में प्राक्षेप मो . रबिऊल कामाल . 
सक्षम प्राधिकारी , इंडियन प्रोयल कारपोरेशन लिमिटेड, हल्दिया -बरौनी क्रूड पाईपलाईन परियोजना , डाकघर - खंजनपक 
बासुदेवपुर , जिला मिदनापुर, पश्चिमी बंगाल को कर सकेगा ।

MINISTRY OF PETRO EUM AND NATURAL GAS 

New Delhi, the 23rd December , 1997 


S. O . 3205. - Whereas, it appears to the Central Government that it is necessary in the public interest 
that for the transport of Petroleum Products from Haldia in the State of West Bengal to Baruni in the State 
of Bihar , a pipeline should be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation Limited ; 


And whereas, it appears that for the purpose of laying such pipelines , it is necessary to acquire the righ 
of user in the land described in the Schedule annexed to this notifica : ion ; 


Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub - section (1 ) of Section 3 of the Petroleum 
and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land ) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962 ), the Central Govern 
ment hereby declares its intention to acquire the right of user therein : 


Any person , interested in the land described in the said schedule may within twenty one days from 
the date on which the copies of this notification , as published in the Gazette of India , are made available 
to the general public, object in writing to the acquisition of the right of user thercin or laying of the Pipelines 
under the land to Shri Md. Rabiul Ramal, competent authoriíy , Indian Oil Corporation Limited , Haldia 
Barauni Pipeline Project, Post Office-Khjananchak , Basudevpur, District-Midnapur (West Bengal).

का . आ . 3206 :- केन्द्रीय सरकार ने , पेट्रोलियम और 
खनिज पाईप - लाईन ( भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन ) 
अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) ( जिसे इसमें इसके 
पश्चात उक्त अधिनियम कहा गया है ) की धारा 3 की 
उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन जारी की गई ओर तारीख 02 / 08 / 97 
के राजपत्र के पृष्ठ सं. 3701 पर प्रकाशित भारत सरकार के 
पेट्रोलियम और प्रकृतिक गैस मंत्रालय की अधिसूचना संख्या 
का . आ . 1895 , तारीख 01 / 08 / 97 द्वारा पेट्रोलियम 
( कुड- आयल ) के परिवहन के लिए पाइप लाइन बिछाने के 
प्रयोजनार्थ उक्त अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमि में 
उपयोग के अधिकारों के अर्जन के अपने आशय की घोषणा की थी ; 

और उक्त राजपत्रित अधिसूचना की प्रतियाँ जनता को 
तारीख 16 / 08 / 97 को उपलब्ध करा दी गई थीं ; 

और उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा (1 ) के 
अनुसरण में सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने केन्द्रीय सरकार को अपनी रिपोर्ट दे

का . आ . 3207 . -- केन्द्रीय सरकार का विहित प्राधिकारी द्वारा उसे प्रस्तुत की गई रिपोर्ट पर विचार करने के पश्चात, समाधान हो गया है कि उक्त 
रिपोर्ट में वर्णित माडल (नीचे दी गई आकृति ) बाट और माप मानक अधिनियम, 1976 ( 1976 का 60 ) और बाट और माप - मानक ( माडल का अमुमोदन ) 
नियम, 1987 के उपबंधों के अनुरूप है और इस बात की संभावना है कि वह लगातार प्रयोग की अवधि में यथार्थता बनाए रखेगा और विभिन्न परिस्थितियों 
में उपयुक्त सेवा देता रहेगा; 


अत : केन्द्रीय सरकार , उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 36 की उपधारा ( 7 ) द्वारा प्रदत शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, ( 1 ) न्यू मल्टी सन्नी - एक्स 
( चूषण टाइप ) टाइप सं . एडीएटी 242; ( 2 ) लम्बा होज मल्टी ( चूषण टाइप ) टाइप सं. एडीटीटी 242 और एडीटीटी 362 और ( 3 ) नॉन - स्पेस टाइप से.

9 . I heard the Learned Advocates for both 
the parties and porsued the record . It is tried to 
submit on W alf of the uniori thai thu Tribunal 
has to answer the reference in the affirmativo 
or in tho negative , as i! is referred to it for 
adjudication . 

10 . So far as the factual position is concerned 
there is no disputo that in view of the decision 
given by His Lordships in both the Writ Petitions 
tle caployecs Inuntioned in the reference are 
to ba absorbed , as per their Lordships order. 

11. Adjudicatio : does not necessarily mean 
answering the reference in the afirmative or in 
the ncgative . It has to be answered as per the 
circumstance . In the present case adınittedly the 
wilt peticions were filed before the High Court of 
Bombay for almost same and similar reliefs as 
made in the reference . Those reliefs were granted . 
The result is that no hing has remainod to be 
adjudicated by this Tribunal. If the reference is 
answered in the affirmative it mcans that the relief 
which is yraut by the Tribunal is again 
reiterated by the Tribunol. There is no need 
to do so , In the result I pass the following 
order : 

ORDER 
In view of the decision givon in writ petitions 
Nos. 367 of 1996 and 369 of 1996 the reference 
becomes infructuous. 

S. B . PANSE , Presiding Officer

cer of the bank . 

6 . The concerned workman has stated that he 
was engaged as peon and had worked from 27 - 11 
1991 to 5 - 10 - 1992 . Whereas Satish Kumar Thaku . 
ral has stated that the concerned workman had 
never worked which is born out from the record . 
The opposite party has relied upon Ext. W - 2 a 
letter dated 11 - 2 -1994 sent by its Manager in reply 
to notice of K , K . Singh Advocate. It appears that 
the concerned workman had served a notice through 
his advocate . By this reply the bank had informed that 
the matter is being looked into . From this it is 
sought to be inferred that the management had admit 
ted about the concerned workman being an empi 
loyee of the bank , I do not agree with this conten 
tion . By this reply simply receipt of notice has been 
acknowledged wih the assurance that the claim of 
cuncerned workman will be examined . It does not 
by any strech of imagination would mean that the 
management had admitted the fact about the con 
cerned workman being the employee of the bank . 
Thus after discarding this locument there is oath 
against oath . In my opinion , the bald statement of 
the concerned workman is not enough . If he was 
paid through vouchers he ought to have got sum 
moned to those papers. In its absence , I do not 
accept the evidence of concerned workman and come 
to the conclusion that the concerned workman was 
not the employee of the bank . Hence question of 
termination breach of provisions of Section (s ) 25 -F , 
25 -G and 25- H of I. D . Act does not arise.

8 . The learned aciy .rute for the petitioner argues that even 
if the complaint Ex. 11 15 is taken into consideration , al 
the amounts mentioned therein were credited in the ledger 
and the petitioner cannot be discharged from service simply 
on the evidences of two witnesses examined in the enquiry 
who identified the initials of the petitioner in the Page Book . 
Both the witnesses are not working in the Branch at the rele 
vant time. The version of the complainant is that the pass 
books is with the Bank only and so the petitioner need not 
make the entries in th : pass book if he wanted to misappr ) 
priate the amount. He also argued that complainant was 
working as Trafic Inspector in APSRTC and his wife was 
working as Staff Nurse , that they entrusted the work of 
depositing the aniount to their son and that the son could 
have manipulated the entries in the Pass Book . On the other 
hand the learned advocate for the Bank argues that the notitin 
ner received the amount of Rs, 5001- every month from the de 
rositor that he uscd the amount for himself and ultimately 
deposited the amount when the fraud was detected . So the 
entries in the Pass Book and the Ledger are not tallying. 
He also argued that the petitioner himsolf remitted the 
amount of Rs. 1500 / - with his signaturo on 19 -8 - 1991 as 
can be seen from Ex, MIO and it proved his misconduct . 


It can be seen that cxcept the entry dt. 9 - 2 - 91 which is the 
first cleposit and the entry dated 27- 8 -91 the other cntries 
are not tallying . The evidences of two officers of the Bank 
is that initials on Ex. M7 Pass Book in Loken of receiving 
the amount are that of the petitioner . Both the officers 
have not worked when the transactions have taken place , 
They were working in other offices . Though the complain 
ant stated in Ex. M16 that he handed over the amounts to 
the petitioner for crediting into aocount, he gave the details 
in Ex . M15. He stated therein that he handed over 
Rs. 1500 / - to the petitioner on 19 - 8 -91, for crediting the 
amount into the Bank . This amount was credited into Bank 
by the Petitioncr as per Ex . M10 remittance challan , He 
next stateal that his son remitted Rs. 500 /- on 24- 8 -91 and 
Rs. 500 / - on 7 - 9 .91. Both the entries are there in the led 
ger , is deposited by Ravikumar ( son ) as per Ex , Mil and 
Ex . M12 remittance challans, The last item of the remit 
tancc by the complainant hiinself is Rs. 1000 - which find s 
a place in the Ledger. He himself mentioned in Ex. M13 
remittance challan that he remitted this amount for the 
months of August and September , 1991. No doubt the 
petitioner remitted the amount of Rs. 1500 / - on 19 - 8 -91 
for the months of March , April and May, 1991 whereas 
the entries of remittance of Rs. 500 ! . On 19 - 9 - 91 and 
Rs. 1 ,000 / - on 19 - 5 -91 . But all the amounts mentioned in 
Ex. M1S were accounted for in the Ledger . 

10 . Only the incriminating circumstances against the peti 
tioner is his initials in Ex. M7 Pass Book . Two officers 
deposed that the initials are that of the petitioner and the 
petitioner having received the money remitted the same as 
per his conveniance . Both the witnesses have not worked in 
The Branch during the relevant period . They have joined 
the branach subsequently . The Branch Manager who was 
working in the Branch at the relevant time is not cxamined 
in the enquiry . 

11 . There is some force inconsistency or improbability in 
the version of the complainant as well as the Bank . The 
complainant says that he left the Pass Book in tlic Bank 
itself and so the petitioner need not makc entries in the 
pass book , if he does not remit the amount into the Bank 
on the days he was said to have received the cash from the 
party. He can make the entries to suit his convenicnce and 
in accordance with the entries in the ledger. Ho has to 
make entries in the past book and put the initials only when 
he handed over the pass book to the customers or the com 
plainant or his nominee as and when the amount was deno 
sited . The Bank did not file the register by name " Over 
night pass book return register " , though asked by the peti 
tioner . This register contains the particulars of pass books 
that are retained in the Bank itself, instead of handing over 
it to the party as and when deposit is made . So the version 
of the complainant that the pass book was in the Bank 
itself is not provod, The two oicers examined in the en . 
quiry are not handwriting experts . The petitioner cannot be 
discharged from service simply on their evidence that the 
initials in the pass book are that of petitioner particularly 
So , when the complainant is not supporting the version of the 
Bank and his version corroborates the ledger entries. The 
Pass Book would be in the complainant s house itself and 
his son Ravi Kumar could have manipulated the entries in 
the Pass Book as the parents were employed and busy and 
not depositing the amounts directly . In these circumstances, 
I hold that the discharge of the petitioner from service is not 
justifiable . However , the petitioner does not have good pre 
vious record . As an employee of the Bank he ought not to 
have taken responsibility of remitting the amount of 
Rs. 1500 / - on 19 - 8 - 91 as evidenced by Ex, M10 . The denial 
of backwage s would be sufficient punishment to the reti. 
toner ,

misappropriated amount of Rs, 14 -10 / - deducted from the 
wages of the cmployees. In our case there is no proof of 
misappropriation . He next relied upon the decision of 
ALLAHABAD BANK AND ANOTHER VS, DEEPAK 
KUMAR BHOLA ( 1997 ( 4 ) SCC Pago 1 ) wherein the 
Supreme Court confirmed the suspension of a Bank emp 
loyee for committing of forgery of 14 signatories and wrong 
fully withdrawing of money from the Bank . In our case , 
the misconduct of the misappropriation is not proved . He 
next relied upon the decision of TARA CHAND VYAS

OTHERS ( 1997 ( 4 ) Supreme Court cases 5651 wherein the 
Branch Manager derelicated in his duty in making payment 
of loan without ensuring supply of implements to loantes 
and deposit of adequate security from dealers. This deci 
sion does not apply to the facts of this case .

Sri M . C . Chandran Nambiar , represented by the General 
Secretary , State Batik of Travancoro Staff Union , Trivan 
drun , regarding the justifiability of the action of the man 
agement in non -desiguating the workman as Head Security 
Guard of Kannur Civil Station Branch of the bank thereby

5 . At the request of the union , notice was issual to the 
present Head Security Guaril of the Kannur Civil Station 
Dianch , Sri D). Surcadian . On receipt of the notice ho 
entored aptrance and filcd a statement supporting the man 
uitment. The mon filecl a rcply statement denying the 
illegations in the statement of Surendran . 


6 . The following poirits arise for consideration : 
( 1 ) Whether the workman is entitled to be designated 

as Icad Schurity Guud of Civil Station Branch , 

Kannur of the managemcnt bank ? 
(2 ) Whether the cost o Head Security Guard is desig 

nated on the basis of centre-wisc weniority or on

7 . No oral cvidence was adduced either by the union , 
management or the worker Surendran , Exts . W1 to W8 were 
marked for the union . On the side of the management Exts , 
M to M6 were marked . 


2 . The union in its clain statement contended thus ;-- - 
In the branches of thic menugement bank with currency chest 
or safe deposit lockers , security guards are posted and where 
there are three or more permanent security guards the senior 
most among them is deswated as Head Security Guard 
who will be entitled to a special allowance of Rs. 203 per 
month . Till September 1993, there were no permanent 
security guards in the Kannur Civil Station Branch of the 
management bank . 4 security guards including the workman 
wers posted by truster at that branch on September 1993 , 
The workman was the seniorniost among the security guards. 
Thereforo he is entitled to be designated as Head Security 
Guard of that branch and he was clesignated as such by the 
Branch Manager by order dated December 18 , 1993 . The 
Branch Manager informed the workman on December 20 , 
1993 that the order posting of him as Head Security Guard 
is kept in abeyanco uuder instructions from Head Office . 
Thereafter the Branch Manager issued an order on March 
16 , 1994 designating another security guard Sri D . Surendran 
who is junior to thc v, orkman as Head Security Guard . The 
above action of the management is illegal and has caused 
much prejudice to the workman . Tho cuse of the manage . 
ment was that the Head Security Guards are dcignated on 
the basis of Centrowise seniority which is the practice fol 
lowed . But the centre-wise seniority is applicable only to an 
existing centre , but not to a centre when it is created . The 
post of security guards were created only in September 1993 
in Kannur Civil Station Branch . Therefore the above prac 
tico is not applicable to that branch . An employee does not 
have any control over the date of relief from his existing 
branch . That being so , if the above mentioned practice of 
centre -wise seniority is followed an employee much junior 
to another employee will be entitled to an undue benefit. 
In the prerent case the orders of transfer to the security 
guards were issued on September 7 , 1993 to the respect v : 
Branch Managers by the Regional Manager. As Mr. Suren 
dran was working at the main branch , Kozhikode he received 
the order on that date itself and joined at Kannur Civil 
Station Branch on September 9 , itself . As Sri Chandran 
Nambiar was working at Mattannur Branch he was relieved 
only on September 22 . The management denied the benefit 
of Head Security Guard to the workman only because he 
was a member of the union . Therefore an award may he 
Dassed holding that the workman is entitlcd to the rost of 
Head Security Guard at the Civil Station Branch . Kannun 
of the management Bank . and that he is entitled to the 
Saarin allowance admissiblc to the post from September 23, 
1993 onwards . 


8 . Point No. 2 . - - The undisputed facts in brief are these 
In State Bank of Travancore , security guards are posted at 
Head Office, Zonal Office and all branches with currency 
chest or yafe deposit luchers . At branches and offices were 
there are 3 or more pulmanent security guards the senior 
most among them is designated as Head Security Guard 
who will be entitled to a special allowance of Rs. 263 per 
month . According to the union , the security guard who is 
senior-most in scrvice is entitled to be designated as Head 
Security Guard of that branch . The management as well as 
Surendran would contend that the Head Security Guards are 
posted on the basis of centrewiso seniority ic . on the basis 
of seniority in the date of entry in a particular centre of 
the bank . 


9 . Therefore the question for consideration is whether the 
seniority in service or seniority at tho centre has to be con 
sidered in designating the Head Security Guard at a parti 
culur branch of the management bank . It is admitted by the 
union that at present the practice followed is that the Head 
Security Guard will be designated on the basis of centre -wise 
seniority . The Secretary of the union argued that such prac . 
tice is not applicable to a new centre where the post of 
security guards are created . l find no merit in the abovo 
contention of the union . The union was not able to point 
out any special circumstance to deviate from the present 
practice of cle iguating the Head Security Guard on the basis 
of centre -wise seniority . 


3 . The management in its statement contended thus : 
The security guards are posted at Head Omce, Zonal Office 
and Branches having currency chest. Security guards are 
not posted in branches where only safe deposit lockers are 
available . Head Security guards are posted on the basis of 
centre -wise seniority . It is true that the orders transferring 
the security guards to Kannur Civil Station Bronch were issu 
ed on September 1 , 1993, Mr. Surendran relieved carlier 
and joined duty on September 9th itself while the worker 
joined only on September 23rd . The date of entry of worker 
at Kannur Civil Station Branch being subsequent to Surendran , 
Surendran has to be considered as senior among the security 
fuords at that centre. Therefore. ho is entitled to be desig 
nated as Head Security Guard of Kannur Civil Ştation Branch , 
It was hy mistake the workman was earlier designated as 
Head Security Guard . It to not correct 10 FAV that in the 
case of a new centre i different rule applies for designating 
A Hend Security Guord . Seniority at the centre is determinerſ 
on the basis of rcrorting of joinine at the centre . This 
practice is followed by the hank on the basis of the decision 

olen at Bipartite Sollement of the union and the manage 
ment. It is not true to say that the workman was victimised 
As he belongs to the union . Therefore. An award may be 
porced tipholding the validity of the action of the manage 
ment. 


10 . The Secrçlary of tlic union argued that if the principle 
of centre -wise seniority is accepted in designating the Head 
Security Guard it will cause miscarriage of justice as in the 
present case . The transfct order of the security guards to 
the Civil Station Branch of Kannur was issued on September 
1 , 1993 as evidenced by Ext. Mi, the copy of the order of 
van fer dotęc Scptember 1, 1993 issuel by the Assistant

h s hle le relieve earlier and join at Kannur Civil

tontion of the union cannot be accepted for the following 


4 . The union filed 3 rejoinder denying the allegations in 
the statement of management and further contending thus :--- 
It is not correct to say that the security guards are posted in 
branches having currency chest alone . There is no rule ,

reasons. The present practice followed in all the branches 
of the bank where there are more than 3 security guards 
are posted is that the Head Security Guard will be desig 
nated on the basis of centre -wise seniority. There is no 
conipelling reason shown before me to deviate from the above 
practice. If the above practice is now discontinued it will 
cause liardship to all the Security Guards working in other 
branches of the bank . That apart the union has not been ablo 
lo show any rulc govcming the designation of Head Security 
Guard on the basis of seniority in service nor was any deci. 
gion in this aspect brought to my notice . Having regard to 
all the these , I am inclined to hold that the present practice 
of the management dosignating the Head Security Guard on 
the basis of centre-wise seniority has to be followed .

11 . Point No . 1 : - - It is the admitted case that the work 
man took charge in the Civil Station Branch of Kannur 
only on September 23 , 1993 while the worker Surendran 
on Scptember 7 , 1993. In view of my above findings, the 
workman iy not entitled to be designated as Head Security 
Guard and the worker Mr. Surendran is entitled to be desig 
nated as Head Security Guard of Civil Station Branch 
Kannur of the management Bank as he is the senior -most 
security guard at that centre .

workman is not entitled to any cf the reliefs claimed .

13 . In the result, the reference is answered holding that 
the action of the management in non -desigpating the work 
man as Head Security Guard of Civil Station Branch of 
Kannur thereby denying special allowance to him is justified . 
It follows that the workman is not entitled to any of the 
reliefs claimed . An award is passed rejecting the claim of 
tho union .

(7 ) When the disciplinary proceedings ends in favour 

of the employee , the employer has often to pay 
back wages, by for about 5 years without being 
able to take work from the omployee concord 
od . On the otherband the omployee concerned 
would had to suffes economic misery and mental 
torture for all those years. Even tho misery of 
being obliged to remain idle without work would 
constitute an unbearable burden . And when 
the curtain drops everyone is left with a bi.ter 
taste in the mouth . All because extreme penalty 
of dismissal or removal is imposed instead of a 

lighter one. 
( 8 ) Every harsh order of removal from service croates 
bitterness and arousos a feeling of antagonism in 

the collective mind of the workers and gives riso 
to a feeling of class conflict. It does more harm 
than good to the employer as also the society . 


( 9 ) Taking of a petty article by a worker in a 

moment of weakness when he yields to a tem 
ptation does not call for an extreme penalty of 
dismissal from service. Most particularly when 
he does not hold a sensitive post of trust 
( pilferage by a casheir or by a store -keeper from 
the stores in his charge , for instanco , may be 
viewed with scriousnese ) . A worker brought up 
and living in an atmosphcic of poverty and want 
when faced with temptation , ought not , but may, 
yield to it in a moment of weakness. It cannot 
be approved , but It can certainly bo understood 
particularly in an age, when even the rich com 
mit economic offences to get richer and do sy 
by and larpe with impugn ty . ( and ( ven tax 
evasion or possession of black money is not 
considered to be dishorouirable by and la * go ), 
A penalty of removal from service is therefore 
not called for when a poor worker yields to a 
momentary temptation and commits an offenco 
which often passes under the honourable name 
of cleptomania when committed by the rich .

In CHIEF GENERAL MANAGER , STATE BANK OF 
INDIA VS. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL & ANR ( 1996 II ) 
LLN P 1183 ) our High Court hold ; 
" On the facts of this case , the Tribunal was not justified 

in seiung aside the order of lermination . The mis 
appropriation was by a bill collector in a post 
wnoso duty way to collect from the office ibe 
amount of pension receivable by the constituents 
of the bank and deposit the same in their accounts , 
Admittedly , the workman failed to deposit several 
thousands of rupees for some months, and mis 
appiopriated the amounts though iemporarily , lbo 
causc put torth was that his creditors compelled 
him to part with the cash after he collec .ed the 
monies from the post office . The debts were said 
to have been incurred for the construction of his 

house . 
Indebtedness is not unoommon , as most peoplo bor 

row monies, for short or long terms tos various 
purposes. Employers , with a view to help their 
employeos also provide advances of various kinds , 
including advance for house building purposes. The 
excuse put forth by the employee that he had to 
misappropriate the funds of the pensioner -cons 
tituents of tho bank to satisfy his creditors , can 
hardly be regarded as a justification , or instigation 
for the gross misconduct committed by him . The 
THbunal has acted perverely in setting aside the 
penalty on the basis of such explanation on the 
part of the workman . The award of compensation 
cquivalent to four ycars salary is equally perverse , 
Not only has the Tribunal condoned the gross mis 
conduct, but further proceeded to reward such gross 
misconduct. The relief of compensation awarded 
by the Tribunal is therefore , set aside and tlie ter 
mination of employment of the second respondent 
uphold . All terminal benefits such as provident 
fund and gratuity to which the workman is entitled 
by reason of his long years of service in the bank 
sha l, however, be paid to him , it has not been paid 

so far ." 
In DHARMAPURI DISTT . CO -OP , SUGAR MILLS VS, 
LABOUR COURT, VELLORE ( 1997 I LLN P 391) at page 
397 , our High Court held ; 
" A careful analysis of the judgements cited by Sri 

N .GR . Prasad only suggests that in casos of minor 
misconduct like the use of abusivo language or acts 
amounting to losą of confidence in the management, 
the respective manavement should not resort to tho 
punishment of dismissal One can easily see the 
line of thinking of the Supreme Court of India in 
relation to the minor and maior misconducts. It is 
lime to remind ourselves about the three choroes 
heid proved by the Labour Colit itself. The first 
charge relates to neglioence in this nerformance 
of duties. raisino considerable emhrassement to the 
management. This charge hvitself mav Amount 
only to loss of confidence , but the second charge 
relates to dishonesty nnd temporary misappropria 
tion . It was sought to be argued that temporary 
misandropriation cannot be equated with thoft. It 
mav he so . But the intention of the worker and 
his general Attitude are clearly visible from the 
proof of the said charge. The third charac rela 
tra to the demand of hority of Rs. 100 from one 
Rolagundaram and Rs. 2001- from P . K . Nate an . 
This it in our view is a very serlous chasre and 
could undermine the very raputation of the manage 
ment. We are of the oninion that when the Court 
js faced with the charm . all of which have been 
nonvert hv evidence adilucent hefore the Labour 
Court itself , it would be improper to have any mis 
placed gympathy in favour of the worker . The 
onestion of rehabilitation would only result in the 
destruction of discipline and morality in the entiro 
factory , Sec . 11A of the Aot was not cortainly 
intended to CA16C such an embarragement to the 
management. In other words , we are of the opinion 
that tho view , expressed by the Labour Court in 


The Learned counsel for tho respondent contended that a 
person in the banking industry who is capablo of misappro 
priating and forging signature of the other person should not 
be roinstatod since he will be tempted again to misappro 
priato other s money . In support of his contention , Learned 
counsel for the respondent cited the following decisions of 
the Hon ble Supreme Court and the Hon ble High Courts . 
THE MUNICIPAL COMMITTEE , BITADURGARH Vs. 
KRISHNAN BEHARI & ORS. ( 1996 II SCC P 714 ) Apex 
Court held as follows : 
" It is obvious that tho respondent has heen crnvicted 

in a serious crime and it is a clear case attracting 
under proviso ( a ) to Article 311 ( 2 ) of the Cons 
titution . In a case of such nature , indeed , in cases 
involving corruption — there cannot be any other 
punishment than dismissal. Any sympathy shown 
in such case is totally uncalled for and opposed to 
public interest. The amount misappropriated may 
be small or large : it is the act of misappropriato 
that is relavant, The Director nad interfered with 
the punishment under a total misapprehension of 
the relevant factors to be borno in mind in such a 


CASO : 


In ALLAHABAD BANK & ANR V9. DEEPAK KUMAR

heid ; 
" What is an offenee involving moral turptitude must 

d -nend unor the facts of each case . But whatever 
mav he the meaning which may bo given to the 
term moral turnrituda it appears to us that onc 
of the most serious offences involving (moral tuisp 
titude ) would be where a person employed in a

its concluding portion , in relation to the punish 
ment, can never be $ usatined as views which a 
reasonahle person can tako. In other words , the 
views of the Labour Court which we have already 
extracted in our judgement can only be charac 
terised as " perverse " . Various judgements of the 
Supreme Court cited above do give power to 
the High Courts to interfere with the impugned 
order of the Labour Court. In this view of the 
matter, while upholding the indings of the Labour 
Court, on charges, we set aside the order of the 
Labour Court, in so far as the punishment portion 

is concerned ." 
Since my predecessor has already held that the enquiry 
conducted against the petitioner is fair and proper and the 
findings are also proper, I am not ablo to go into tho merits 
of tho enquiry conducted against the petitioner . However, 
the culpability of the Manager in giving a withdrawal form 
after office hours and making payment of money after office 
bours and making entries on the next day will throw & 
doubt on the part played by the Manager and the cashier 
who filled up tho challan in this transaction . However , the 
workman (petitioner ) has not filed any appeal or writ 
against the finding of this Tribunal with regard to the fair 
ness of the domestic enquiry . But this aspect can be con . 
sidered at the time of evaluating whethor the punishment 
given to the petitioner is justifiable . 

The learned counsel for the respondent submitted that a 
person who has misappropriated money should not be em 
ployed in the services of a bank where money belonging to 
customers are handled . Learned counsel for the petitioner 
comparing the case of one C . P . Sarathi, who has mis . 
appropriated more than 9 .0001- (more than 6 times than the 
amount misappropriated by the petitioner ) has been lot of 
with a lenient punishment of stoppage of two increments 
whereas the petitioner has been terminated from service . 
The Loarned Counsel for the respondent has submitted that 
the petitioners case cannot be compared with the care of 
Sarathi because C . P . Sarathi admitted his guilt from the 
very boginning and pleaded for mercy wheroes the petitioner 
hag contested tho charge against him . Because the petitioner 
has contested the charge against him , It cannot be said that 
he should be awarded the maximum punishment when the 
misconduct committed by him is far lesser than the mis . 
conduct committed by C . P . Santbl who was let off with 
a lonient punishment of stoppage of two increments only . 
Judgement of the Division Bench of the Gujarat High Court 
referred carlier has clearly hold that an emplovee fucing a 
proceeding which could result in his economic death has a 
right to contect and regist it and he is not bound to admit 
the charges or to plead guilty in order to enable him to 
invotre iurisdiction of the Court uls. UA of the ID . Act, 
to reduce the punishment. When comnaring with the mis 
conduct committed hy C . P . Sarathi, and punishment indic 
ted on him , the petitioner has to be dealt with leniently and 
mercy should be shown to him . Termination of sevice is 
the maxmum punishment which could cause economic 
death . The petitioner is without employment for the last 
17 years . Taking into consideration of all these aspects , I 
hold that the termination of service of the petitioner is 
unſustified and he should be reinstated in service with 
50 per cent of back wages and other attendant beneits . 

In the result , award is passed holding that the action of 
the Management of Vysya Bank Ltd ., in terminating the 
sorvices of Shri K . Swami Naidu , Ex. Sub -staff is not legal 
and he is entitled to reinstatement with 50 per cent back 
wages and other attendant benefits. No costs . 
Dated , this the 29th day of October, 1997 . 

S . ASHOK KUMAR , Industrial Tribunal

5 . The number of days the workman had worked in differ 
ent periods are given by the management when they filed 
counter statement before a Conciliation Officer after the 
raising of this dispute . Ext. W2 is the reply given by the 
management before the Conciliation Officer on 25 - 4 - 1994 
From page 2 of Ext. W2 it can be seen that during ninc 
months period, that is from 20 - 3 - 1990 to 31 - 12 - 1990 , the 
workman had worked 204 days. In the year 1991 he had 
worked for 201 days. From 1 - 1 - 1993 to 30 -6 - 1993 he had

Pandalam Branch and Elaviimthitta Branch . It is the ad 
mitted case of the management that since he was engaged 
on daily basis, no payments wer given to him on Sundays 
and other holidays. On the other hand the workman s cage 
fe that he had worked allocother 556 days of servico in 
between 1 - 3 - 1992 to 9 - 11 - 1993 and that was in addition to

These details are given in Ext. W1 statement filed by him 
before the Assistant Labour Commissioner, Trivandrum diu 
ing the pendency of conciliaiton proceedings. It is the case 
of the workman that whenover he worked , he was being paid 
wages through vouchers and custodian of those signed you 
chere is the management Bank . In order to prove his caso , 
he filed a petition before this Tribunal on 10 - 1- 1997 with 
the plea for giving direction to the management to proluce 
Charges Account Register maintained at Pandalam . Elaviim 
thitta and Kulnpada branches of the mananement during 
the periori from March 1990 to November 1993 . Plça was 
also made to produce all the debit glipe /vouchers corres 
ponding to the entries in the charges accmint register main 
tained at Pandalam , Elrumthitta and Kulanada hranchos 
for the above bald neriod. In response to that petition , tho 
Managemont had produced only the registers of the year 
1993 and the Billg, vouchers of that period . The reason for 
non -production of required register and debit sling for the 
entire period from March 1990 to December 1992 was ex 
plained through a memo fled before this Court while no . 
ducing available documents . It is stated therrin that charoes 
amount account and debit glin in reanect of only January 
1903 to July 1993 were syailable . The eynlanation plyen 
bv the management for the fin -nroruntinm of milar docuie 
menta like that of Pat. M3. M4 and M5 and Fat M7 are 
not convincing. because those were the most relevant dou 
ment for the proper determination of this dispute , In this 


connection it is to be noted that this dispue was raisod 
immediacly after the alleged termination of the workman . 
Ext, WI statement showinx th¢ actails of service was thed 
by the workman immediately after the starting of concilia 
tion procedings . knowing well about such contentions in 
advance , the management ought to have preserved the rele 
vant documents w vindicato their stund . Thereforo their 
failure to produce required materials will only vindicate the 
stand of the workman that he was in continuous employment 
for about four years . It is also the admitted case of both 
sides that wlienicver he was employed , he was being paid 
on da. ly basis thiough vouchers . All the vouchers duly 
signed by him after receipt of money would have been the 
proncr matcrial to ascertain the number of days he had 
worked during the period in question . Therefore benefit 
of presumption on account of non - furnishing of relevant 
materials before this Tribunal has to be given to the work 
man , in these circumstances, this Tribunal has no option but 
10 conclude that the woskinan had rendered 240 days of 
wontinuous service immcdiately procceding the date of his 
ulleged termination . 

6 . From the materials furnished by the management them 
Selves (Ext. W2), it can lyc seen that during the period of 
9 months in the vcar 1990 , the workinan had worked for 
204 says. Thercfore there was every possibility to his 
continuince in service more than 240 days in every year 
starting from 20 - 3 - 1990 . In that case he is entitled for 
notice Day 21 !! ompensation on the basis of number of 
years of service he l endered immediately beforo the alleged 
cate of termination . 

7 . The plca of the worlman that he is entitled for absorp 
tion or regularisailon in service cannot be upheld , becaure , 
apnointment to the stall and sub -stuff category is to be done 
in accordance with the Rules and Regulations framed by the 
Central Government and the bank managemont from time 
10 time. For making icgular appointment to the sub -stall 
category , sponsoring, of name from the Employment Exchange 
ig essential and admittedly the workman was not sponsored 
ly the Employment lxchange at any time. Only on ceitl. 
fication by the Employment Exchange that there was no 
qualified candidate in the list maintained by the Employment 
Exchange , direct appointment by the Bank Management i: 
possiblo in the sub- staff category . Therefore there is no 
sustainable ground for the regularisation of workman in der 
vico of the management Bank . However in view of the 
conclusion by the Tribunal that he had worked for more 
than 240 days of service inimediately before the alleged dato 
of termination , he is entitled for protection as envisaged in 
Section 25- F of the ID : Act. Admittedly the management: 
had not complied with that mandatory prov sions at the time 
of termination . Hence it is declared that workman is ontitl 
ed for protection as envisaged in Section 25 - F . Till a validt 
termination is effected by the management, he is entitled 
for the benefit of continuity in service as if there was no 
termination . As a conscqucnice he is entitled for full wages 
also in the nianner as he was getting on the date immodiately 
before the alleged date of termination . 


Award is passed accordingly ,

S . O . 3251. ---In exercise of the powers con 
ferred by Section 4 of the Employees State 
Insurance Act, 1948 ( 34 of 1948 ) , the Cen 
tral Government hereby makes the following 
amendment in the notification of the Govern 
ment of India in the Ministry of Labour No . 
S. Q . 509 ( E ) , dated the 8th June, 1995 pub 
lished in the Gazette of India, Extraordinary, 
Part- II , Section 3 ( ii ) dated the 8th June , 
1995 . 


And whereas, the Central Government is 
of opinion that public interest requires the ex 
tension of the said period by a further period 
of six months%3 

Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers 
conferred by the proviso to sub -clause ( vi) of 
clause ( n ) of section 2 of the Industrial Dis 
putes Act, 1947, the Central Government here 
by declares the said industry to he a public 
utility service for the purposes of the said Act, 
for a period of six months from the 18th Dec 
ember, 1997.
